# Bubble-tea shop simulation v2 — complete result tables
Generated by `analysis/build_report_v2.py` from `results/v2/*.json`. Every number is read from a result file; the HTML report at [docs/index.html](index.html) contains the curated tables and figures.

## scenario_library

Source file: `results/v2/scenario_library.json` (regenerated by the experiment script).

### provenance
| key | value |
|---|---|
| `experiment` | scenario_library |
| `schema_version` | 1 |
| `package_version` | 2.0.0 |
| `python_version` | 3.14.5 |
| `engine` | bubble_tea_sim.engine.Simulation (stdlib only) |
| `notes` | One block per scenario in data/scenarios/. Provenance is copied from the JSON file; config.describe() and the layer description come from the loaded SimConfig. 100 replicates per scenario, first seed 0, stride 1. Flow conservation and finiteness are asserted for every replicate, so a scenario that fails would abort this script instead of appearing here. The balk decision uses the engine's wait estimate divided by the number of staff units ON SHIFT at the arrival instant (shift windows and breaks are coverage-checked), so scenarios with late shift starts, breaks or a mid-shift joiner are not credited with absent staff; the stationary legacy path keeps the full worker count so the v1 anchors stay bit-identical. Note: sla.fairness_cap_minutes in a scenario file is an alias for the policy starvation cap, so peak_pricing/strict_sla_fairness behave as their provenance describes. |
| `reps` | 100 |
| `seed` | 0 |
| `seed_stride` | 1 |
| `scenario_count` | 16 |
| `total_replicates` | 1600 |
| `elapsed_seconds` | 33.7000 |

## demand_study

Source file: `results/v2/demand_study.json` (regenerated by the experiment script).

### provenance
| key | value |
|---|---|
| `experiment` | demand_study |
| `schema_version` | 1 |
| `package_version` | 2.0.0 |
| `python_version` | 3.14.5 |
| `engine` | bubble_tea_sim.engine.Simulation (stdlib only) |
| `notes` | Layer 1 experiments. Scenarios come from data/scenarios/; every other cell is a dataclass override of campus_rush or baseline_flat. Cells whose estimated peak load exceeds 0.95 use 60 replicates, the rest use 100 (first seed 0, stride 1). Paired differences use common random numbers on identical seeds. Variability and patience cells use a two-segment unit demand profile so the fixed-service cell also runs on the v2 event-driven dispatch path (a single flat segment would silently use the legacy path and confound the comparison). The balk decision compares the customer's threshold with the engine's wait estimate divided by the number of staff units ON SHIFT at the arrival instant; these cells have no shift windows, so it equals the worker count, but the library scenarios with late shift starts and breaks are not credited with absent staff. |
| `reps_default` | 100 |
| `reps_overload` | 60 |
| `seed` | 0 |
| `seed_stride` | 1 |
| `elapsed_seconds` | 87.3000 |

## network_study

Source file: `results/v2/network_study.json` (regenerated by the experiment script).

### provenance
| key | value |
|---|---|
| `experiment` | network_study |
| `schema_version` | 1 |
| `package_version` | 2.0.0 |
| `python_version` | 3.14.5 |
| `engine` | bubble_tea_sim.engine.Simulation (stdlib only) |
| `notes` | Layer 2 experiments on the network scenarios. Station utilisation comes straight from the engine; labour-pool utilisation is derived by grouping the stations that share a skill and dividing their busy minutes by the pre-break shift minutes of the staff that can operate them. Cells with an estimated peak load above 0.95 use 60 replicates (the saturated tea capacities), everything else uses 100; first seed 0, stride 1. The balk decision uses the engine's Little's-Law-style wait estimate divided by the number of staff units that are ON SHIFT at the arrival instant (shift windows and breaks are coverage-checked), so a late-joining barista does not make the shop look bigger than it is; the legacy v1 path keeps the full worker count so the golden anchors stay bit-identical. |
| `reps_default` | 100 |
| `reps_overload` | 60 |
| `seed` | 0 |
| `seed_stride` | 1 |
| `elapsed_seconds` | 73.2000 |

**`note`**

critical_capacity is the smallest capacity whose extra station fails to pay for itself (marginal profit/h <= 0 on common random numbers).
